```
> (e828386, runtime-forced, pre-run, Michael GO, instrument-side ONLY — register/gates/verdicts/a-priori
> UNCHANGED): STYLE-MATCHED SYMBOLIC ANCHORS.** The frozen spec named crystal_probes() anchors, but crystal
> primitives are ~entirely NATURAL LANGUAGE ("The cat cleaned itself"=I) vs terse SYMBOLIC compounds
> ("S K K x") → style confounds function, ASYMMETRICALLY favoring the already-favored OPERATIONAL
> (false-negative risk on the surprising EXTENSIONAL); fix = symbolic saturated anchors, CL5 void-gate
> measured on them in the alignment pool (crystal s217 z=7.97 = external ref). **✅ HARNESS

> reduce(compound)==reduce(NF-primitive) on same atoms (the CL identity, kernel-proven); --validate 4
> planted worlds (EXTENSIONAL/OPERATIONAL/SYNTACTIC/VOID) ALL PASS; ruff clean; no diags; qwen3-4b smoke
> green (CL5 z=10.78, verdict not read).** **✅ RUN LANDED (cb3fdd3 autonomous, read-only ~45s, tmux main:1,
> 426 probes): VERDICT MIXED-REDUCTION-VISIBLE (pre-reg tree) → mechanism QUALIFIED-OPERATIONAL/SYNTACTIC.**

> paired NF>OP p=0.0515 (marginal miss) → pass=False. CL2 within-NF coh 0.112 < token-matched null 0.174
> p=0.70 → FAIL (coherence alphabet-driven not NF-driven; W spellings anti-cohere). **THE READ (decisive
> per-row split, don't over-read the MIXED label): the positive mean-NF is a LITERAL SYMBOL-PRESENCE
> artifact.** DIRTY spellings (NF-symbol present/fired: KII, S(KI)I, BIB) nf +0.280 vs CLEAN dissociating
> spellings (NF-symbol ABSENT: SKK, SKS, WK, CKK, SS(KI), CSI, S(KS)K) nf **−0.031**. Where the dissociation
```

```
### The discriminating structure (same trick as SD1: a pre-registered sign)

"Weights keep growing" is trivially true (norm growth ≡ generic
optimizer physics) — so the accumulation view must predict something
DIFFERENTIAL: growth in a fixed shared window concentrated on
consistent-signal (early-frozen) coordinates, absent on contested
(churning) ones.

- Window: b11→b19 (steps 1k→143k) — post-freeze for the ENTIRE
  early-frozen population by construction, identical for all coords.
- Per-coord growth g = log(|W_b19|+ε) − log(|W_b11|+ε).
- Populations: early-frozen (freeze_bin ≤ b10) vs churners (≥1 flip
  after b15), matched within pooled |W_b11| deciles (kills
  growth-rate-is-a-function-of-current-size; weight decay biases
  AGAINST accumulation ⇒ conservative).
- AT1 statistic (pinned): Δ = Σ_k w_k · (mean g_early,k − mean
  g_churn,k) over qualifying deciles k (w_k ∝ n_k); medians reported
  alongside, not gated. Null = within-decile population-label
  permutation, 10k draws.
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""§P-AMP-TRAJECTORY — accumulation-vs-uniform-growth re-read of strata.npz.

Pre-reg: mementum/knowledge/explore/types-are-a-modulation-scheme.md
§P-AMP-TRAJECTORY (FROZEN s325, Michael GO — frozen BEFORE any trajectory
statistic was computed; SD1/SD2 read signs + final magnitudes only).

Claim (Michael, s325, on the INVERTED verdict): the lattice concentrates by
ACCUMULATION — amplitude ∝ ∫consistent-signal over the whole run; contested
cancels to net≈0. Rival 1: UNIFORM norm growth (generic optimizer physics —
"weights keep growing" is trivially true, so the claim must be DIFFERENTIAL).
Rival 2: EROSION (residual self-erasure flavor: late writes route away from
committed coords).

Statistic (pinned): fixed shared window b11→b19 (steps 1k→143k, post-freeze
for the whole early-frozen population by construction); per-coord
g = log(|W_b19|+ε) − log(|W_b11|+ε); early-frozen (freeze≤b10) vs churners
(≥1 flip after b15) matched within pooled |W_b11| deciles; AT1 Δ = weighted
mean over qualifying deciles (≥500 each pop) of (mean g_early − mean g_churn);
within-decile label-permutation null ×10k. Medians reported, not gated.
Weight decay biases AGAINST accumulation ⇒ conservative.

Verdicts + a-priori (NOT tuned; modal on the null):
ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION 30 / UNIFORM-GROWTH 40 / EROSION 20 / VOID 10.

License: MIT (lambda provenance).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

_HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
if str(_HERE) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(_HERE))

from stratigraphy_dating import (  # noqa: E402
    CHURN_MIN_BIN,
    COMMONS_MAX_BIN,
    EPS,
    N_BINS,
    observables,
)

WINDOW_START = 11              # step 1000
WINDOW_END = 19                # step 143000
MIN_PER_DECILE = 500           # decile qualifies iff >=500 of EACH population
MIN_QUAL_DECILES = 3
N_PERM = 10_000
ALPHA = 0.05
APRIORI = {"ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION": 30, "UNIFORM-GROWTH": 40,
           "EROSION": 20, "VOID": 10}


def _pooled_deciles(x: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Decile labels 1..10 over the pooled vector x (rank-based)."""
    order = np.argsort(x, kind="stable")
    r = np.empty(len(x), float)
    r[order] = np.arange(len(x), dtype=float)
    return np.minimum((r / len(x) * 10).astype(int) + 1, 10)


def gate_at0(obs: dict, pop: np.ndarray, quals: list[int]) -> dict:
    valid = obs["valid"]
    n = max(int(valid.sum()), 1)
    n_early = int((pop == 1).sum())
    n_churn = int((pop == 2).sum())
    ok = (float(valid.mean()) > 0.99 and n_early / n >= 0.01
          and n_churn / n >= 0.01 and len(quals) >= MIN_QUAL_DECILES)
    return {"gate": "AT0", "pass": bool(ok), "n_early": n_early,
            "n_churn": n_churn, "n_qualifying_deciles": len(quals),
            "qualifying_deciles": quals}


def gate_at1(g: np.ndarray, pop: np.ndarray, dec: np.ndarray,
             quals: list[int], rng: np.random.Generator,
             n_perm: int = N_PERM) -> dict:
    if not quals:
        return {"gate": "AT1", "delta": 0.0, "p_pos": 1.0, "p_neg": 1.0,
                "sign": "ns", "per_decile": {}}
    per, wts, med = {}, [], {}
    pools = []
    for d in quals:
        m = dec == d
        ge, gc = g[m & (pop == 1)], g[m & (pop == 2)]
        per[str(d)] = float(ge.mean() - gc.mean())
        med[str(d)] = float(np.median(ge) - np.median(gc))
        wts.append(len(ge) + len(gc))
        pools.append((np.concatenate([ge, gc]), len(ge)))
    w = np.array(wts, float)
    w /= w.sum()
    delta = float(np.dot(w, [per[str(d)] for d in quals]))
    null = np.empty(n_perm)
    for i in range(n_perm):
        acc = 0.0
        for wk, (pool, k) in zip(w, pools, strict=True):
            p = pool[rng.permutation(len(pool))]
            acc += wk * (p[:k].mean() - p[k:].mean())
        null[i] = acc
    p_pos = float((null >= delta).mean())
    p_neg = float((null <= delta).mean())
    sign = "positive" if (delta > 0 and p_pos < ALPHA) else \
           "negative" if (delta < 0 and p_neg < ALPHA) else "ns"
    return {"gate": "AT1", "delta": delta, "p_pos": p_pos, "p_neg": p_neg,
            "sign": sign, "per_decile_mean": per, "per_decile_median": med}


def gate_at2(obs: dict, mags: np.ndarray, pop: np.ndarray) -> dict:
    """Advisory: fraction of final amplitude present at sign-freeze."""
    sel = np.where(pop == 1)[0]
    if len(sel) == 0:
        return {"gate": "AT2", "advisory": True, "n": 0}
    fb = obs["freeze_bin"][sel].astype(int)
    r = mags[fb, sel] / (mags[N_BINS - 1, sel] + EPS)
    q = np.percentile(r, [25, 50, 75])
    return {"gate": "AT2", "advisory": True, "n": len(sel),
            "R_q25": float(q[0]), "R_median": float(q[1]),
            "R_q75": float(q[2]), "frac_R_below_half": float((r < 0.5).mean())}


def verdict(at0: dict, at1: dict) -> str:
    if not at0["pass"]:
        return "VOID"
    if at1["sign"] == "positive":
        return "ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION"
    if at1["sign"] == "negative":
        return "EROSION"
    return "UNIFORM-GROWTH"


def run_gates(signs: np.ndarray, mags: np.ndarray, rng: np.random.Generator,
              n_perm: int = N_PERM):
    obs = observables(signs)
    valid = obs["valid"]
    fb = obs["freeze_bin"]
    pop = np.zeros(signs.shape[1], np.int8)      # 0 other, 1 early, 2 churn
    pop[valid & (fb <= COMMONS_MAX_BIN)] = 1
    pop[valid & (fb >= CHURN_MIN_BIN)] = 2
    g = (np.log(mags[WINDOW_END] + EPS) - np.log(mags[WINDOW_START] + EPS))
    both = pop > 0
    dec = np.zeros(signs.shape[1], np.int8)
    dec[both] = _pooled_deciles(mags[WINDOW_START][both])
    quals = [d for d in range(1, 11)
             if (both & (dec == d) & (pop == 1)).sum() >= MIN_PER_DECILE
             and (both & (dec == d) & (pop == 2)).sum() >= MIN_PER_DECILE]
    at0 = gate_at0(obs, pop, quals)
    at1 = gate_at1(g, pop, dec, quals, rng, n_perm=n_perm)
    at2 = gate_at2(obs, mags, pop)
    return [at0, at1, at2], verdict(at0, at1)


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# --validate: planted worlds
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
def _mk_world(kind: str, rng: np.random.Generator, n: int = 20_000):
    sf = rng.choice([-1, 1], n).astype(np.int8)
    signs = np.repeat(sf[None, :], N_BINS, axis=0).astype(np.int8)
    half = n // 2
    early = np.arange(half)
    churn = np.arange(half, n)
    # early: frozen from b1 (b0 random)
    signs[0, early] = rng.choice([-1, 1], half).astype(np.int8)
    # churn: alternate every bin from b1 through b18 => flips after b15
    for b in range(1, N_BINS - 1):
        signs[b, churn] = signs[b - 1, churn] * -1
    # magnitudes: same |W_b11| distribution for BOTH pops (deciles overlap)
    m11 = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)).astype(np.float32)
    g = np.zeros(n, np.float32)
    if kind == "ACCUM":
        g[early], g[churn] = 1.0, 0.0
    elif kind == "UNIFORM":
        g[:] = 0.5
    elif kind == "EROSION":
        g[early], g[churn] = 0.0, 1.0
    elif kind == "VOID":
        signs[:, churn] = np.repeat(sf[None, churn], N_BINS, axis=0)  # no churners
        g[:] = 0.5
    mags = np.zeros((N_BINS, n), np.float32)
    t = np.arange(N_BINS, dtype=np.float32)[:, None]
    frac = np.clip((t - WINDOW_START) / (WINDOW_END - WINDOW_START), 0, 1)
    mags[:] = m11[None, :] * np.exp(frac * g[None, :])
    mags[:WINDOW_START] = 0.5 * m11[None, :]
    return signs, mags


def validate() -> bool:
    expected = {"ACCUM": "ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION",
                "UNIFORM": "UNIFORM-GROWTH", "EROSION": "EROSION",
                "VOID": "VOID"}
    ok_all = True
    for kind, want in expected.items():
        rng = np.random.default_rng(77)
        signs, mags = _mk_world(kind, rng)
        gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, rng, n_perm=2000)
        ok = v == want
        ok_all &= ok
        at1 = gates[1]
        print(f"  world={kind:<8} verdict={v:<26} want={want:<26} "
              f"delta={at1['delta']:+.3f} {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'}")
    print(f"--validate: {'ALL PASS' if ok_all else 'FAIL'}")
    return ok_all


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--strata", type=Path,
                    default=Path("results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/strata.npz"))
    ap.add_argument("--out", type=Path,
                    default=Path("results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/amp_trajectory.jsonl"))
    ap.add_argument("--validate", action="store_true")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    if args.validate:
        return 0 if validate() else 1

    rng = np.random.default_rng(325)
    z = np.load(args.strata)
    signs, mags = z["signs"], z["mags"]
    t0 = time.time()
    gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, rng)
    rec = {"probe": "§P-AMP-TRAJECTORY (FROZEN s325)",
           "strata": str(args.strata),
           "timestamp": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime()),
           "elapsed_s": round(time.time() - t0, 1)}
    with args.out.open("w") as f:
        f.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\n")
        for gd in gates:
            f.write(json.dumps(gd) + "\n")
        f.write(json.dumps({"verdict": v, "a_priori": APRIORI}) + "\n")
    for gd in gates:
        print(json.dumps(gd))
    print(f"VERDICT: {v}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

### Thinking

I'm seeing the core issue now: the AT1 delta metric conflates two distinct dynamics. Early-frozen coordinates show consistent growth and accumulation, while churners are actively canceling or shrinking their engagement. Both movements contribute to the overall delta, but they're fundamentally different stories that need to be separated in the analysis.

To disentangle this, I need to introduce a middle population as a decile-matched baseline—those who aren't early-frozen and aren't churners—then measure growth relative to that middle group and cancellation relative to it separately. This gives me two independent signals: whether early-frozen outperforms the baseline, and whether churners underperform it. ### Thinking

I'm mapping out the bin structure with 20 total bins where early bins use smaller step sizes (512 at b10, 1000 at b11) and later bins follow a half-decade progression up to 143,000 at b19, with the churn analysis starting from bin 16 and distinguishing between early-frozen populations that committed before the window opened and active churners.

The middle population—those who froze during bins 11–15—committed within the measurement window itself, so their growth metrics include a pre-commitment churn phase that serves as a baseline for generic physics with intermediate consistency. The key distinction is whether growth means early-frozen coordinates accumulate more than this baseline (genuine signal accumulation) or whether churners grow less (contested cancellations where their net stays flat while everything else grows).

For the statistics, I'm computing two weighted means across deciles: growth as the difference between early and middle populations' mean growth, and cancellation as the difference between middle and churn populations. Both use deciles pooled across all three populations based on |W_b11|, with a decile qualifying for a comparison only if it contains at least 500 samples from each population in that pair, requiring at least 3 qualifying deciles per comparison. I should also check whether the growth and cancellation differences sum approximately to the overall AT1 difference when using the same decile structure.

Now I'm thinking through the verdict framework: the AT2 heterogeneity results suggest both growth and cancellation effects are present—25% of early populations shrink while 34% show doubling—which aligns with the revision's picture of edges accumulating into the lattice while contested regions cancel to near-zero. The median R of 0.78 indicates substantial amplitude even at freeze, and given the 25% shrinkage rate, the most likely scenarios are either both effects significant or growth-only with cancellation nonsignificant.

I'm also considering the alternative cases: cancellation-only would mean early-frozen populations are just generic growers and the AT1 win came entirely from churner suppression; neither effect significant seems unlikely given the large sample size unless the middle population behaves like one extreme rather than sitting between them; and inverted cases where early populations grow *less* than middle would be a surprising branch worth naming and tracking separately.

For the verdict structure, I'm thinking a clean 2×2 grid based on whether growth and cancellation effects are significant, with categories like BOTH, GROWTH-DRIVEN, CANCELLATION-DRIVEN, and UNSEPARATED, plus a VOID case. When a result has the wrong sign but is still significant—meaning the ordering assumption breaks—I should flag that as MIDDLE-NOT-INTERMEDIATE rather than forcing it into the main grid, keeping the pre-registered decision tree crisp.

Now I'm laying out the specific gates: the middle population needs to be non-degenerate (at least 1% of valid cases), with at least 3 qualifying deciles per comparison, and I'm noting an advisory about middle ordering. The growth gate checks if the growth effect exceeds baseline at p<0.05, and the cancellation gate does the same for cancellation. Looking at the verdict distribution across a sample, I see BOTH-LIVE cases dominating, with GROWTH-DRIVEN and CANCELLATION-DRIVEN splitting the remainder, a smaller UNSEPARATED group, and a few VOID cases—but I'm still working through where the mass should actually concentrate.

The data's pushing back: SD2 shows churners heavily concentrated at small weight magnitudes (73% in the bottom decile), which statistically suggests cancellation is real. AT2's median R of 0.78 indicates early growth is modest for the median but 34% of cases show doubling or more. Generic growth norms would predict the middle grows too, so any early extra growth needs a genuine accumulation signal to stand out. Michael's revision leans toward BOTH, but I'm questioning whether that's the right frame—the λ yardstick points toward the null or mundane arm. Cancellation-driven might actually be the mundane read here, since contested nets zeroing out is standard SGD behavior and early coordinate behavior isn't inherently special; AT1's effect seems driven mainly by churner suppression rather than something novel.

I'm settling on a probability distribution: BOTH and CANCELLATION-DRIVEN each at 30%, GROWTH-DRIVEN at 15%, UNSEPARATED at 15%, and VOID at 10%. The revision hinges on whether accumulation is actually happening—the deflationary rival is pure cancellation with no growth. Making them co-modal at 30 reflects that tension. There's also a confound in how the middle population is defined: middle coordinates commit during blocks 11–15, so early-window growth might include pre-freeze churn that looks small initially then grows later. This biases the middle growth estimate downward, which conservatively underestimates the growth signal and also conservatively underestimates the cancellation effect.

Actually, wait—if middle coordinates were still churning at the window start, their baseline is small, then after commitment they grow substantially. That could flip the bias: middle growth goes up, which makes it easier to show churners below baseline, anti-conservatively favoring the cancellation gate. The early growth estimate would be affected differently.

I'm considering restricting the middle baseline to coordinates that committed early in the window—specifically those with freeze_bin around 11 or 12—since they'd have minimal rebound-growth confounding. Let me check what the step values are around that range. I'll set up the GC gates with middle bins 11–15 as the primary gated condition (capturing the natural middle ground between early and churner patterns), then run a separate advisory robustness check using the tightest middle bins 11–12 to minimize rebound contamination. I'll also add a per-middle-bin breakdown advisory and keep the GC3 within-early-frozen split to population-level reporting. For consistency, I'll report how the combined Δ_g + Δ_c compares to AT1's Δ, noting they won't match exactly due to different decile qualification and weighting. The VOID condition triggers if GC0 fails—meaning a degenerate middle or fewer than 3 qualifying deciles for either comparison.

Now I'm shifting to the decile computation: instead of separate decile frames for early+churn in AT1, I'll pool all three populations (early, mid, churn) to create one shared decile frame based on pooled |W_b11|, which simplifies pairwise comparisons while maintaining within-decile balance for the populations being compared. For the null distribution, I'll run within-decile permutation tests of the pair labels (early vs mid for GC1, mid vs churn for GC2) with 10k draws each. I'll also add an advisory check on the overall mean g ordering to confirm g_early > g_mid > g_churn. Finally, I'm settling the a-priori allocation: Both-Live 30%, Cancellation-Driven 30%, Growth-Driven 15%, Unseparated 15%, Void 10%.

The justification hinges on Cancellation-Driven being the baseline deflationary mode (contested-nets-zero is standard per SD2), so the revision's claim of accumulation above baseline must outperform it. Growth-Driven gets lower weight because it would require churners to behave generically despite churning, which is implausible. Unseparated at 15% accounts for middle contamination that could blur both signals. The Both-Live license is strongest because it requires demonstrating both differential accumulation on committed coordinates and cancellation on contested coordinates against the same baseline, which directly supports the revision. Cancellation-Driven captures AT1's Δ being driven by churner suppression, but the "accumulation" framing is weaker there.

Now examining how the revision's growth clause loses its initial support when re-read—AT1 stands as measured but the synthesis claim that magnitude scales with consistency integration needs requalification since evidence only grows relative to contested space, not the baseline. Growth-Driven assumes accumulation is real while cancellation isn't distinct from baseline, but the contested-equals-cancellation clause licensed by SD2 statics lacks dynamic support. Unseparated leaves the matched design unresolved; AT1 still stands with the split remaining open and possible middle contamination. The bounds stay consistent with the parent models.

Adding the middle population coordinates during the window—rebound contamination that's anti-conservative for GC2 but conservative for GC1. Now I need to draft the freeze section and present it to Michael for approval. ## 🎯 FREEZE PROPOSAL — §P-GROWTH-CANCEL-SPLIT (own-null re-read, strata.npz, zero compute)

**Question.** The ✅ AT1 Δ = +0.975 conflates two mechanisms: early-frozen coords growing *above* baseline (ACCUMULATION) vs churners cancelling *below* baseline (CANCELLATION). AT2 heterogeneity says both may be live. Which carries the Δ?

**The baseline.** Third population MID = valid ∧ freeze_bin ∈ [11,15] (committed steps 1k–16k; neither early-frozen nor churner; n = 60,638). Same window b11→b19, same g = Δlog|W|, same pooled-|W_b11| decile matching — now pooled over all **three** populations, one shared decile frame.

**Statistics (pinned, same trick as AT1 — pre-registered signs, own nulls).**
- **Δ_growth** = Σ w_k (mean g_early,k − mean g_mid,k) — early above baseline
- **Δ_cancel** = Σ w_k (mean g_mid,k − mean g_churn,k) — churners below baseline
- Each: within-decile pair-label permutation null ×10k; decile qualifies iff ≥500 of each population *in that pair*; ≥3 qualifying deciles per comparison. Consistency identity Δ_growth + Δ_cancel ≈ AT1 Δ reported, not gated.

**Gates.**
- **GC0 SANE** — npz loads; three populations non-degenerate (each ≥1% of valid); ≥3 qualifying deciles per comparison.
- **GC1 GROWTH-ABOVE-BASELINE** — Δ_growth > 0 at p < 0.05.
- **GC2 CANCELLATION-BELOW-BASELINE** — Δ_cancel > 0 at p < 0.05.
- **GC3 CONTAMINATION-ROBUSTNESS (advisory, never gates)** — repeat both with MID restricted to fb ∈ {11,12} (n ≈ 13.8k, minimal mid-window commitment rebound) + per-fb baseline breakdown. Report only.

**Verdicts + a-priori (NOT tuned; co-modal on the revision and its deflation).**

| Verdict | Condition | Mass |
|---|---|---|
| BOTH-LIVE | GC1 ✓ ∧ GC2 ✓ | 30 |
| CANCELLATION-DRIVEN | ¬GC1 ∧ GC2 ✓ | 30 |
| GROWTH-DRIVEN | GC1 ✓ ∧ ¬GC2 | 15 |
| UNSEPARATED | neither (incl. wrong-sign sig) | 15 |
| VOID | GC0 fail | 10 |

CANCELLATION-DRIVEN is co-modal deliberately: contested-nets-to-zero is standard SGD lore and SD2's statics (churners ≡ small) already point there — the revision's *distinctive* clause (accumulation above baseline) has to beat that deflationary rival to keep the word "accumulation."

**Read discipline (banked now).**
- **BOTH-LIVE** → both clauses of the revision licensed vs same-substrate baseline; §Synthesis "magnitude ∝ ∫consistency" strengthened at this register.
- **CANCELLATION-DRIVEN** → AT1's Δ was carried by churner suppression; the revision's growth clause LOSES its first-contact support (AT1 stands as measured, re-read); §Synthesis magnitude-clause requalified to "contested cancels" only.
- **GROWTH-DRIVEN** → accumulation real; dynamic cancellation not distinct from baseline (SD2 statics stay, dynamics unsupported).
- **UNSEPARATED** → matched design lacks resolution here; AT1 stands; split stays open.
- Nothing here rescues §2 (dead on its own sign) or touches the original frame's 0–2 ledger.

**Confound named (not an escape hatch).** MID commits *during* the window → post-commitment rebound growth from a cancellation-depressed |W_b11| inflates g_mid ⇒ **conservative for GC1, anti-conservative for GC2**. That asymmetry is why GC3 exists: if GC2 passes only in the full-MID read and dies at fb∈{11,12}, the cancellation reading is flagged contamination-suspect in the §Result (advisory, pre-committed language).

**Bounds.** Inherits §P-STRATIGRAPHY-DATING/§P-AMP-TRAJECTORY bounds verbatim (pythia-160m, register-ABSENT lineage, MLP band, ordinal bins).

**Pre-freeze disclosure.** Only sign-register population counts (fb histogram — data class fully read s325) were inspected for feasibility; no magnitude-trajectory statistic of the MID population has been computed.

### Thinking

The commit's in, so I'm moving on to task 3—building the harness script for growth cancellation splits with validation on planted worlds. I'm reusing the observables and constants from the stratigraphy dating module, and structuring the decile machinery similarly to the amplitude trajectory approach but adapted for three populations: early adopters with feedback below 10, churned users at feedback 16 or higher, and the middle segment in between. The run_gates function will compute observables from the sign data, partition populations by their feedback thresholds, and then calculate a log-transformed magnitude metric.

Now I'm defining the gate conditions: GC0 checks that all three populations represent at least 1% of valid data with at least 3 qualifications per comparison and overall validity above 99%. GC1 tests for growth by comparing early versus mid populations with a positive delta and p-value below 0.05. GC2 tests for cancellation by comparing mid versus churn with the same significance threshold. GC3 is advisory, repeating both tests with mid restricted to feedback values 11 and 12, computing per-feedback group means, and checking consistency between the growth and cancellation deltas against an amplitude trajectory-style early versus churn comparison. The verdict gets classified as BOTH-LIVE, CANCELLATION-DRIVEN, GROWTH-DRIVEN, UNSEPARATED, or VOID based on which gates pass. Wrong-sign significance counts as a gate failure, so both wrong-sign results fold into UNSEPARATED rather than triggering separate classifications—matching the frozen tree logic where CANCELLATION-DRIVEN requires GC2 to pass even if GC1 is significantly negative.

For the planted worlds validation, I'm constructing sign patterns where early is frozen from bin 1, churn alternates through bin 18, and mid flips until reaching a freeze point around bin 12, then stays constant. The freeze bin is determined by finding the first bin where the sign remains constant for all subsequent bins—so if signs differ at the bin 11 to 12 transition but stay the same from bin 12 onward, the freeze bin is 12.

I'm setting up three test worlds with different gate configurations: one with early and mid gates passing (both-live scenario), one with only early gate passing (growth-driven), and one with early and mid gates at intermediate values (cancellation-driven). Half the mid-world instances will have freeze bin 12 and half at 14 to test the GC3 subset breakdown.

Then I'm adding two edge cases: an unseparated world where all gates are at 0.5, and a void world that fails the early gate by having no mid population. For the magnitude construction, I'm using a lognormal base magnitude scaled by exponential growth factors across populations, with a pre-window baseline at half the base. The mid-world coordinates flip signs during the window, but the magnitudes stay consistent since we're validating the statistical machinery rather than physical realism. I'm considering whether to add small noise to the growth factors to avoid zero variance within pools, since the current deterministic approach might make the permutation null test too clean.

For the sample size, I'm using 30,000 total across three equal populations of 10,000 each, which gives roughly 1,000 per decile—well above the 500 minimum. The output goes to a jsonl file following the amplitude trajectory pattern, with seed 326. I also need to compute a GC3 variant with restricted mid coordinates and break down mean growth by feedback value.

I'm running a permutation test comparing early versus churn consistency alongside the main comparisons, reporting…

1000\nWINDOW_END = 19                # step 143000\nMID_ROBUST_MAX_FB = 12         # GC3 advisory: MID restricted to fb in {11,12}\nMIN_PER_DECILE = 500\nMIN_QUAL_DECILES = 3\nN_PERM = 10_000\nALPHA = np.arange(len(x), dtype=float)\n    return np.minimum((r / len(x) * 10).astype(int) + 1, 10)\n\n\ndef _pair_delta(g: np.ndarray, pop: np.ndarray, dec: np.ndarray,\n                a: int, b: int, rng: np.random.Generator,\n                n_perm: int = N_PERM) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Weighted within-decile Δ = mean g_a − mean g_b + pair-label perm null.\"\"\"\n    quals = [d for d in range(1, 11)\n             if ((dec == d) & (pop == a)).sum() >= MIN_PER_DECILE\n             and ((dec == d) & (pop == b)).sum() >= MIN_PER_DECILE]\n    if len(quals) < MIN_QUAL_DECILES:\n        return {\"n_qualifying_deciles\": len(quals), \"delta\": 0.0,\n                \"p_pos\": 1.0, \"p_neg\": 1.0, \"sign\": \"ns\", \"quals\": quals}\n    per, med, wts, pools = {}, {}, [], []\n    for d in quals:\n        m = dec == d\n        ga, gb = g[m & (pop == a)], g[m & (pop == b)]\n        per[str(d)] = float(ga.mean() - gb.mean())\n        med[str(d)] = float(np.median(ga) - np.median(gb))\n        wts.append(len(ga) + len(gb))\n        pools.append((np.concatenate([ga, gb]), len(ga)))\n    w = np.array(wts, float)\n    w /= w.sum()\n    delta = float(np.dot(w, [per[str(d)] for d in quals]))\n    null = np.empty(n_perm)\n    for i in range(n_perm):\n        acc = 0.0\n        for wk, (pool, k) in zip(w, pools, strict=True):\n            p = pool[rng.permutation(len(pool))]\n            acc += wk * (p[:k].mean() - p[k:].mean())\n        null[i] = acc\n    p_pos = float((null >= delta).mean())\n    p_neg = float((null <= delta).mean())\n    sign = \"positive\" if (delta > 0 and p_pos < ALPHA) else \\\n           \"negative\" if (delta < 0 and p_neg < ALPHA) else \"ns\"\n    return {\"n_qualifying_deciles\": len(quals), \"quals\": quals,\n            \"delta\": delta, \"p_pos\": p_pos, \"p_neg\": p_neg, \"sign\": sign,\n            \"per_decile_mean\": per, \"per_decile_median\": med}\n\n\ndef gate_gc0(obs: dict, pop: np.ndarray, growth: dict, cancel: dict) -> dict:\n    valid = obs[\"valid\"]\n    n = max(int(valid.sum()), 1)\n    counts = {\"early\": int((pop == EARLY).sum()),\n              \"churn\": int((pop == CHURN).sum()),\n              \"mid\": int((pop == MID).sum())}\n    ok = (float(valid.mean()) > 0.99\n          and all(c / n >= 0.01 for c in counts.values())\n          and growth[\"n_qualifying_deciles\"] >= MIN_QUAL_DECILES\n          and cancel[\"n_qualifying_deciles\"] >= MIN_QUAL_DECILES)\n    return {\"gate\": \"GC0\", \"pass\": bool(ok), **counts,\n            \"n_quals_growth\": growth[\"n_qualifying_deciles\"],\n            \"n_quals_cancel\": cancel[\"n_qualifying_deciles\"]}\n\n\ndef gate_gc3(g: np.ndarray, pop: np.ndarray, dec: np.ndarray, fb: np.ndarray,\n             rng: np.random.Generator, n_perm: int = N_PERM) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Advisory: MID restricted to fb in {11, MID_ROBUST_MAX_FB} + per-fb g.\"\"\"\n    pop_r = pop.copy()\n    pop_r[(pop == MID) & (fb > MID_ROBUST_MAX_FB)] = 0\n    growth_r = _pair_delta(g, pop_r, dec, EARLY, MID, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    cancel_r = _pair_delta(g, pop_r, dec, MID, CHURN, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    per_fb = {str(b): {\"n\": int(((pop == MID) & (fb == b)).sum()),\n                       \"mean_g\": float(g[(pop == MID) & (fb == b)].mean())\n                       if ((pop == MID) & (fb == b)).any() else None}\n              for b in range(COMMONS_MAX_BIN + 1, CHURN_MIN_BIN)}\n    return {\"gate\": \"GC3\", \"advisory\": True,\n            \"n_mid_restricted\": int((pop_r == MID).sum()),\n            \"growth_restricted\": {k: growth_r[k] for k in\n                                  (\"delta\", \"p_pos\", \"p_neg\", \"sign\",\n                                   \"n_qualifying_deciles\")},\n            \"cancel_restricted\": {k: cancel_r[k] for k in\n                                  (\"delta\", \"p_pos\", \"p_neg\", \"sign\",\n                                   \"n_qualifying_deciles\")},\n            \"mid_per_fb_mean_g\": per_fb}\n\n\ndef verdict(gc0: dict, gc1: dict, gc2: dict) -> str:\n    if not gc0[\"pass\"]:\n        return \"VOID\"\n    g_ok = gc1[\"sign\"] == \"positive\"\n    c_ok = gc2[\"sign\"] == \"positive\"\n    if g_ok and c_ok:\n        return \"BOTH-LIVE\"\n    if c_ok:\n        return \"CANCELLATION-DRIVEN\"\n    if g_ok:\n        return \"GROWTH-DRIVEN\"\n    return \"UNSEPARATED\"\n\n\ndef run_gates(signs: np.ndarray, mags: np.ndarray, rng: np.random.Generator,\n              n_perm: int = N_PERM):\n    obs = observables(signs)\n    valid = obs[\"valid\"]\n    fb = obs[\"freeze_bin\"]\n    pop = np.zeros(signs.shape[1], np.int8)\n    pop[valid & (fb <= COMMONS_MAX_BIN)] = EARLY\n    pop[valid & (fb >= CHURN_MIN_BIN)] = CHURN\n    pop[valid & (fb > COMMONS_MAX_BIN) & (fb < CHURN_MIN_BIN)] = MID\n    g = (np.log(mags[WINDOW_END] + EPS) - np.log(mags[WINDOW_START] + EPS))\n    dec = np.zeros(signs.shape[1], np.int8)\n    all3 = pop > 0\n    dec[all3] = _pooled_deciles(mags[WINDOW_START][all3])\n\n    growth = _pair_delta(g, pop, dec, EARLY, MID, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    cancel = _pair_delta(g, pop, dec, MID, CHURN, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    at1_replica = _pair_delta(g, pop, dec, EARLY, CHURN, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n\n    gc0 = gate_gc0(obs, pop, growth, cancel)\n    gc1 = {\"gate\": \"GC1\", **growth}\n    gc2 = {\"gate\": \"GC2\", **cancel}\n    gc3 = gate_gc3(g, pop, dec, fb, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    consistency = {\"gate\": \"CONSISTENCY\", \"advisory\": True,\n                   \"delta_growth_plus_cancel\":\n                       float(growth[\"delta\"] + cancel[\"delta\"]),\n                   \"at1_replica_delta\": at1_replica[\"delta\"],\n                   \"at1_replica_sign\": at1_replica[\"sign\"]}\n    return [gc0, gc1, gc2, gc3, consistency], verdict(gc0, gc1, gc2)\n\n\n# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# --validate: planted worlds\n# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef _mk_world(kind: str, rng: np.random.Generator, n: int = 30_000):\n    \"\"\"Three planted populations: early (fb<=10), mid (fb 12/14), churn.\"\"\"\n    sf = rng.choice([-1, 1], n).astype(np.int8)\n    signs = np.repeat(sf[None, :], N_BINS, axis=0).astype(np.int8)\n    third = n // 3\n    early = np.arange(third)\n    mid = np.arange(third, 2 * third)\n    churn = np.arange(2 * third, n)\n    # early: frozen from b1 (b0 random)\n    signs[0, early] = rng.choice([-1, 1], len(early)).astype(np.int8)\n    # mid: alternate through b11 (first half -> fb=12) or b13 (-> fb=14)\n    mid_a, mid_b = mid[: len(mid) // 2], mid[len(mid) // 2:]\n    for b in range(1, 12):\n        signs[b, mid_a] = signs[b - 1, mid_a] * -1\n    for b in range(1, 14):\n        signs[b, mid_b] = signs[b - 1, mid_b] * -1\n    # churn: alternate through b18 => flips after b15\n    for b in range(1, N_BINS - 1):\n        signs[b, churn] = signs[b - 1, churn] * -1\n    # planted growth per population over the window\n    grow = np.zeros(n, np.float32)\n    if kind == \"BOTH\":\n        grow[early], grow[mid], grow[churn] = 1.0, 0.5, 0.0\n    elif kind == \"GROWTH\":\n        grow[early], grow[mid], grow[churn] = 1.0, 0.0, 0.0\n    elif kind == \"CANCEL\":\n        grow[early], grow[mid], grow[churn] = 0.5, 0.5, 0.0\n    elif kind == \"UNSEP\":\n        grow[:] = 0.5\n    elif kind == \"VOID\":\n        # no mid population: freeze mid from b1 like early\n        signs[:, mid] = np.repeat(sf[None, mid], N_BINS, axis=0)\n        signs[0, mid] = rng.choice([-1, 1], len(mid)).astype(np.int8)\n        grow[:] = 0.5\n    # same |W_b11| distribution for all pops (deciles overlap by construction)\n    m11 = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)).astype(np.float32)\n    mags = np.zeros((N_BINS, n), np.float32)\n    t = np.arange(N_BINS, dtype=np.float32)[:, None]\n    frac = np.clip((t - WINDOW_START) / (WINDOW_END - WINDOW_START), 0, 1)\n    mags[:] = m11[None, :] * np.exp(frac * grow[None, :])\n    mags[:WINDOW_START] = 0.5 * m11[None, :]\n    return signs, mags\n\n\ndef validate() -> bool:\n    expected = {\"BOTH\": \"BOTH-LIVE\", \"GROWTH\": \"GROWTH-DRIVEN\",\n                \"CANCEL\": \"CANCELLATION-DRIVEN\", \"UNSEP\": \"UNSEPARATED\",\n                \"VOID\": \"VOID\"}\n    ok_all = True\n    for kind, want in expected.items():\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(77)\n        signs, mags = _mk_world(kind, rng)\n        gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, rng, n_perm=2000)\n        ok = v == want\n        ok_all &= ok\n        gc1, gc2 = gates[1], gates[2]\n        print(f\"  world={kind:<7} verdict={v:<20} want={want:<20} \"\n              f\"dg={gc1['delta']:+.3f} dc={gc2['delta']:+.3f} \"\n              f\"{'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'}\")\n    print(f\"--validate: {'ALL PASS' if ok_all else 'FAIL'}\")\n    return ok_all\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--strata\", type=Path,\n                    default=Path(\"results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/strata.npz\"))\n    ap.add_argument(\"--out\", type=Path,\n                    default=Path(\"results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/growth_cancel_split.jsonl\"))\n    ap.add_argument(\"--validate\", action=\"store_true\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    if args.validate:\n        return 0 if validate() else 1\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(326)\n    z = np.load(args.strata)\n    signs, mags = z[\"signs\"], z[\"mags\"]\n    t0 = time.time()\n    gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, rng)\n    rec = {\"probe\": \"§P-GROWTH-CANCEL-SPLIT (FROZEN s326)\",\n           \"strata\": str(args.strata),\n           \"timestamp\": time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime()),\n           \"elapsed_s\": round(time.time() - t0, 1)}\n    with args.out.open(\"w\") as f:\n        f.write(json.dumps(rec) + \"\\n\")\n        for gd in gates:\n            f.write(json.dumps(gd) + \"\\n\")\n        f.write(json.dumps({\"verdict\": v, \"a_priori\": APRIORI}) + \"\\n\")\n    for gd in gates:\n        print(json.dumps(gd))\n    print(f\"VERDICT: {v}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n"
```json
}
```

### Thinking

Both models are live, but the breakdown reveals something important: the growth effect is minimal at just 5.4%, with the first decile actually showing negative movement, while the cancellation effect dominates at 0.922—meaning most of the difference comes from AT1 reducing churn below baseline. The consistency score of 0.054 reflects how small that growth component really is.

Now I'm checking the robustness across different commitment windows. When I restrict to the most recent cohorts (fb 11–12), the pattern flips dramatically: growth turns negative and cancellation grows even larger. The per-cohort growth metrics show a clear downward trend as commitment timing gets later, which initially looks like the contamination pattern I predicted at freeze. But there's a puzzle here—my earlier reasoning suggested that the freshest baseline would be least contaminated, yet those same recent cohorts show the highest growth values. I need to reconsider which subset actually has minimal contamination and what that means for the GC1 interpretation. The full-MID read shows Δ_growth is small and fragile—contamination could plausibly shift it either direction. GC2 passed robustly in both reads, so the cancellation effect holds up. But GC1's growth clause is genuinely brittle, flipping sign under the restricted baseline, which the verdict tree flags as BOTH-LIVE.

The result carries an important caveat: cancellation dominates the effect (95% of the split), while growth is thin and unstable to baseline changes, though the per-fb monotone pattern hints that post-commitment accumulation might actually be visible within the mid population itself—earlier commitment correlates with more growth, which would be consistent with accumulation happening after the commitment point. Early-frozen commits show growth around 0.93 over the window, much lower than the fb=11 mids at 2.38, which seems counterintuitive since both should be fully committed for the entire window—but the early-frozen entries started from a higher base after accumulating 512+ steps of growth beforehand, suggesting log-growth saturation is at play here, a rebound-base effect where the mids are recovering from a depressed starting point. This is interpretive context for the results section, but the key finding is that both live and frozen entries show the pattern.

The statistical breakdown shows early-frozen sits slightly above baseline (growth delta +0.054, p≈0) while churners fall far below (cancellation delta +0.922, p≈0), decomposing into roughly 6% growth and 94% cancellation. The growth effect isn't robust—it flips negative under certain fb ranges—but the cancellation effect holds strong at +1.062. The per-fb runway gradient reveals the contamination is substantial, and the freeze labeling was misleading about which fb values had minimal contamination; the advisory design note corrects this in the results, though the gates themselves remain unaffected. The honest takeaway is still that both live and frozen entries show the effect.

The cancellation-dominated split means the accumulation clause barely survives as a fragile margin above baseline. Committed coordinates sit only slightly above the committed-mid baseline. Now I need to commit the results—the growth and cancellation split data gets written to the jsonl file, and I'll follow the historical pattern of committing the harness separately first, then the results autonomously.…

**The sharp facts:**
- **Churners genuinely shrink** — raw mean g = **−0.42 log units** over 1k→143k while *every* committed population grows. Cancellation is absolute, uniform, and robust to every baseline choice. This is the clause that carried AT1.
- **Committed-extra-growth is thin and fragile** — +0.054 matched, sign-flips under the GC3 restriction, and raw order is inverted (early +0.13 < mid +0.88; base-level/saturation dominates raw growth).

```
ACCUMULATES with use (de Carvalho / quantitative semantics). Idempotent
intersection — the pre-committed death — predicts membership SATURATES at

non-idempotence on the frozen WEIGHT plate; this re-aims it at the tape/ICL

Register (λ measure) = LICENSING, NOT kind-magnitude (heeds the s319 caveat
+ the 3× magnitude-null): L(w,prefix) = mean surprisal(anti preds) −
mean surprisal(own preds), sign fixed by w's true class (tw._signed_L).

Construction: nonce w, class c; prefix carries k∈{0..5} membership exposures.
  COHERENT   arm — k distinct paraphrases of w's TRUE membership (A2 coherent

  INCOHERENT arm — k length/form-matched NON-membership statements about w
                   (energy-matched A2 null; same token budget, no class edge).
Read L(k) per arm; discriminator = slope_coherent − slope_incoherent.

intersection (both establish the type at first exposure). A literal
"ρ(L,k)>0 over all k" IB1 therefore PASSES for an idempotent step-function
(flat after k=1) → IDEMPOTENT would be nearly unreachable, contradicting the
frozen 15% a-priori. Fix: the accumulation gates IB1/IB2/IB3 operate on
**k≥1** (does the license keep growing AFTER the first exposure — the actual
non-idempotence signature A∧A vs A); **k=0 feeds IB4 SANE only** (L(0)≈0,
L(1)>0 = register works). The frozen INTENT ("idempotent saturates after
first exposure" vs "non-idempotent keeps accumulating") is exactly preserved;
this makes IDEMPOTENT genuinely reachable, as the a-priori assumed.

Gates: IB1 ACCUMULATION (slope>0 over k≥1, k-perm null) · IB2
COHERENT-SPECIFIC (slope_coh>slope_inc paired, make-or-break) · IB3
NON-SATURATING (increments k≥2 >0, non-gating corroboration) · IB4 SANE
(void-gate). Verdicts NON-IDEMPOTENT(+NON-SATURATING) / EVIDENCE-ONLY /
IDEMPOTENT / VOID.

REAL_MEMBERS, _signed_L, REAL_MARGIN_FLOOR) + holo_cap (NONCE_CANDS) +

import argparse
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

_HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
if str(_HERE) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(_HERE))
_WRAP = _HERE.parents[1] / "wrapper"
if str(_WRAP) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(_WRAP))

import type_write as tw  # noqa: E402  (frozen §8 harness — constants + pure fns)
from holo_cap import NONCE_CANDS  # noqa: E402

from verbum.dsp.nulls import (  # noqa: E402
    NullDraws,
    Register,
    gate,
    paired_permutation,
    sign_flip,
)

# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Construction (FROZEN §P-IDEMPOTENCY)
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
K_VALUES = (0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5)          # exposure counts (5 = all paraphrases)

# INCOHERENT arm — length/form-matched to tw._member_stmts but MEMBERSHIP-FREE:
# no class word (animal/vehicle), no held predicate, no cohyponym class. Same
# surface skeleton so the token budget matches the coherent arm exposure-by-
# exposure (the A2 energy-matched null: same exposures, no coherent edge).
_INCOHERENT_TEMPLATES = (
    "A {w} is nearby.",
    "The {w} is on the table.",
    "Every {w} was counted.",
    "{w}, like the box and the lamp, is here.",
    "I saw a {w}; it is over there.",
)


def incoherent_stmts(w: str) -> list[str]:
    return [t.format(w=w) for t in _INCOHERENT_TEMPLATES]


def coherent_prefix(w: str, cls_i: int, k: int) -> str:
    if k <= 0:
        return ""
    return " ".join(tw._member_stmts(w, cls_i)[:k]) + " "


def incoherent_prefix(w: str, cls_i: int, k: int) -> str:
    if k <= 0:
        return ""
    return " ".join(incoherent_stmts(w)[:k]) + " "


# words that must NOT appear in the incoherent arm (membership-free guard)
def _forbidden_words() -> set[str]:
    forbidden = set(tw.CLASSES)                          # animal, vehicle
    for preds in tw.HELD_PREDS:
        forbidden.update(preds)
    for members in tw.REAL_MEMBERS:
        forbidden.update(members)
    return forbidden
```

## Retrodictions (the frame earns its keep — these were measured BEFORE the frame)

1. **Non-idempotency = coherent integration.** Phased-array law: k coherent
   exposures → amplitude ∝ k (power ∝ k²); incoherent → √k. §P-IDEMPOTENCY:
   coherent paraphrases accumulate licensing 1→3, energy-matched incoherent
   flat. Not *like* coherent gain — IS A2's coherent gain, on the tape.
   Two substrates (weight-plate s292 + tape licensing s320), one signal law.

2. **∨-costs-more / ∧-free = code-superposition algebra.** CDMA: one token
   carries two codes simultaneously at no structural cost — superposition is
   linear ⇒ **intersection is free**. "Either code" is NOT a linear object —
   union needs machinery outside the span. §P-DISJ-COST measured exactly
   this: OR needs an off-plane direction (p=0.002), AND does not. The
   Cartesian-control asymmetry is a *prediction* of code superposition.

3. **Gradedness = demodulation SNR.** Membership as correlation with a code
   is naturally continuous — the graded, non-Church-tag character (SKI
   control #2 tested-dead) stops being a quirk and becomes forced.

**Negatives become theorems:** you cannot store a demodulation *event* in
the plate (MEMORIZED-ONLY — CE training writes new codewords/predicate
memories but does not extend the demodulator); a spread signal is
readable-everywhere/excisable-nowhere by construction (HRR/VSA crosstalk
~1/√D); a static amplitude read of a compound sees the spelling's carrier,
not the demodulated content (CL-collapse operational verdict).

**Triangulation flag:** the s288 convergence page wrote *"arguments are on
the plate; application is the diffraction"* (functors). The s315–s323 arc —
different instruments, different sessions — landed on *"weights = checker,
tape = judgments."* Same law, derived twice, independently.

## The instrument gap (λ measure, in hindsight)

If types are carrier-borne, every static geometric read queried the wrong
register — a photometer pointed at a radio. The s308 optics meta-lesson
says it directly: *you never untangle a plate by cleverer processing of a
single recorded intensity.* You change recording geometry, take
phase-controlled exposures, sweep the selectivity curve, or close the loop.

## Technique map → probe candidates (UNFROZEN)

### ① Lock-in amplification → §P-TYPE-LOCKIN (cheap; natural first front)

Modulate type evidence on the tape at a known token-periodic rate —
coherent membership statements alternating with incoherent filler in a
fixed pattern — demodulate the per-frame type-register trace at f_mod.
Narrowband detection at f_mod ⇒ judgment is carrier-borne; harmonic content
reads the demodulator's linearity. The presence-detector result is the DC
reading; this is the AC reading. **Reuses the idempotency harness's
coherent/incoherent statement populations (already built, s320).**

### ② Trace coherence → §P-TYPE-COHERENCE (cheap; the signal-domain rescue of extensionality)

CL-collapse read static AMPLITUDE routing: SKK ≠ I. Phase is where
holography hides everything. Measure spectral/trace coherence between
per-frame register signals of `SKK` and `I` ACROSS the reduction trace vs
structure-matched controls. Traces converging into phase-coherence as
reduction proceeds ⇒ extensionality lives in the dynamics — where
tape-resident reduction says it must, and where a static read is blind by
construction. Reuses cl_collapse + trace_fuel machinery.

### ③ Bragg rocking curve → §P-TYPE-ROCKING (medium)

s312 already conjectured *"type = reference angle"* (slot-mediation).
Angle-multiplexed volume holograms have narrow angular selectivity. Sweep
licensing efficiency vs geometric interpolation angle between class
subspaces. Bragg ridge ⇒ types are angle-multiplexed exposures; plateau ⇒
generic. Point-sampling this surface is what made writes inert (s304–s305);
sweeping it is the optics discipline. Instance of the s308 rocking-curve
instrument, specialized to the type register.

### ④ Phase conjugation → §P-CONJUGATE-WRITE (medium; TYPE-WRITE-V3 design)

All type-writes were OPEN-LOOP: minimize CE on membership outputs, hope
the judgment installs. Adaptive optics: measure the wavefront, write its
conjugate. Capture the per-layer residual trajectory during a SUCCESSFUL
ICL type judgment (§11 tag-transit instrument exists), train the wire to
reproduce the INTERNAL wavefront (activation-matching loss on the
tape-side judgment signal), not the output. Installs under conjugate
writing but not CE writing ⇒ we learn WHY every write failed: energy at
the output while the judgment is a mid-stack signal event. Natural
sharpening of the queued §P-COHERENT-WRITE.

### ⑤ Dark-field → boundary-echo re-read (cheap; persisted data)

The s320 boundary-churn 93/6 split: 93% generic centroid-structure ≡ the
zeroth order / DC term. Optics blocks the DC and measures the rest at full
dynamic range. Project out the generic component FIRST, re-measure the
kind-specific ~6% with full instrument power. Re-analysis of persisted
§P-BOUNDARY-CHURN + §P-TYPE-GRAM-1 artifacts; no new compute.

### ⑥ Already in flight — §P-FLIP-CONFLICT is the time-domain arm

Sign-oscillation as sigma-delta multiplexing = "types in the signal" on
the TRAINING axis (temporal multiplexing of superposed codes). G1∧G2 ⇒
the modulation picture gains a training-dynamics leg to go with the
inference-time legs above.

## The novel prediction (falsifiable; distinguishes the frame)

**Lock time.** If judgments are carrier lock, frames-to-license ∝ 1/SNR of
the evidence, with a **capture threshold**: degrade/truncate coherent
evidence and the judgment should FAIL TO LOCK below threshold, not weaken
proportionally. Stored-geometry accounts predict graceful proportional
degradation; demodulation predicts a threshold. No other account on the
table predicts the threshold. (Cheap add-on to §P-TYPE-LOCKIN: sweep
evidence SNR, look for the knee.)

## §2 Differential Photography — the training-side leg (s324, same hammock)

> Michael: "if training is taking probability photographs through backprop,
> the probabilities will concentrate at the edges and corners where the
> snapshots overlap right?" → sharpened by one amendment: **backprop
> photographs the RESIDUAL, not the scene.** Each exposure records only what
> the plate failed to predict; error feedback is negative feedback, so the
> write signal is self-erasing the moment a pattern becomes predicted.

### The law (amplitude is difficulty, not probability)

```
λ differential_photography(training).
  exposure(t) ≡ snapshot(error(t)) ¬snapshot(data)
  | amplitude(pattern) ∝ ∫ error(pattern, t) dt ≈ time_to_learn | ¬∝ P(pattern)
  | common → predicted_fast → faint ∧ early ∧ threshold_written ∧ frozen
  | rare_consistent → wrong_long → deep ∧ late (the exceptions dictionary)
```

```
## The meta-pattern (the s308 optics meta-lesson, generalized)

Every discipline that succeeds at black-box inference uses four moves:

```
λ re_meta(x).
  1 control(input_distribution)      | chosen-plaintext ∨ designed excitation ∨ gated probes ✓
  2 hypothesis_keyed_stats(n_trials) | DPA ∨ GWAS ∨ our nulls ✓ (partition-subtract > correlation)
  3 recognize(known_parts)           | standard cells ∨ spectral lines ∨ index fossils (partial: crystal probes; no LIBRARY discipline yet)
  4 read(history) ¬just(state)       | stratigraphy ∨ dendrochronology ∨ checkpoint series — THE UNMINED MOVE

### Thinking

I'm trying to understand what the per-frame type-register trace measures—it seems to be a token-by-token signal derived from the type-register projection that tracks gate-space centroids along the kind axis as the prompt streams evidence in a periodic pattern, contrasting with the idempotency register which only captures the final surprisal difference. I need to verify the specifics of how this per-token measurement works.

For the actual experiment design, I'm sketching out a lock-in detection setup where I build a long sequence of segments alternating between coherent membership statements and incoherent fillers according to a known binary schedule—starting with a simple periodic modulation at a fixed frequency, then upgrading to a maximal-length LFSR PRBS sequence so I can cross-correlate the register trace against the lagged PRBS pattern to estimate the impulse response.

The signal I'm measuring is the per-segment type-register response—either the projection of hidden gate activations onto the type axis at each segment boundary (cheap, one forward pass) or a running licensing readout (expensive). I'll go with the boundary projection approach, then demodulate by correlating against the modulation schedule and its lags, comparing the lock-in statistic at the target frequency against a null distribution from shuffled schedules.

For the transfer function, I'm using PRBS (either length 127 or 31) to estimate the impulse response via cross-correlation, then taking the FFT to get the transfer function and deriving lock-time from the step response. The novel prediction is an SNR sweep where I degrade the coherent evidence and measure how the lock detection threshold behaves—the knee or failure point below a critical SNR becomes the frame's falsifiable prediction.…

### Thinking

I'm reviewing the available instruments for this analysis: I have persisted centroids from the type-gram model that let me project gate activations onto kind axes for per-token type-register readouts across layers, a tag-transit instrument that captures per-layer and per-position data during ICL type judgment, and an idempotency tool that tracks coherent and incoherent statement populations with licensing readouts. Now I'm setting up to work through the layer-wise analysis.

The core approach is to stream segments of coherent membership statements versus incoherent matched fillers, run a forward pass once, capture gate activations per token in the band layers, then project onto the type register axis—likely the own-class versus anti-class contrast from the centroids—and aggregate per segment to get y(t) for demodulation against m(t). The key design decision is pinning down the register readout: whether to use the mean projection over all segment tokens or just the last token, which type axis to project onto (the animal versus vehicle contrast from the type-gram centroids in the deep layers where the register is most semantic), and confirming the structure of those centroids—the 30 nodes likely represent type kinds, so I need to locate the specific class axis for the animal/vehicle distinction.

For the modulation schedules, I'm considering a PRBS arm using either PRBS-31 or PRBS-127 to alternate between coherent and incoherent segments, with segment lengths around 10–15 tokens fitting comfortably within the context window. The subtlety is that coherent evidence accumulates non-idempotently due to the system's memory, which is exactly what system identification handles through the transfer function and impulse response.

I'm also thinking about adding a periodic square-wave arm at the modulation frequency as a narrowband confirmation alongside the PRBS primary approach, since the PRBS alone gives the full transfer function but a periodic signal provides clean corroboration. For the null condition, I need to shuffle the modulation schedule labels while keeping the forward pass identical, which tests whether the effect is truly driven by the schedule rather than the underlying computation. The lexical confound is that coherent statements inherently contain the class word while incoherent ones don't, so a surface-level response to word identity could masquerade as a coherence effect. I need to either use a contrast where true-class and wrong-class coherent statements have identical surface forms (though nonce words lack ground truth labels), or restructure the analysis so the class distinction itself becomes the primary axis of variation rather than coherence. I'm separating the excitation phase (where evidence segments vary) from the measurement phase (where I read out at a fixed probe token like "The wug is here"), keeping the lexical surface constant at readout so any modulation in the neural response must come from the internal judgment state rather than surface variation.

This creates a clean experimental design where the type-register oscillates with the evidence schedule—the judgment becomes dynamically readable like an AC signal rather than just a static DC presence. I still need the schedule-permutation null for statistical control and a class-word-non-membership control arm to rule out attention artifacts bleeding into the probe activations, especially given what I learned from the earlier lexical confound issues.

Now I'm setting up the control arm with class-word-present non-membership fillers, gating on whether the main detection exceeds the control detection to isolate the membership-specific component. For the novel prediction about lock-time and capture threshold, I'm designing an SNR sweep that degrades evidence coherence by replacing or diluting coherent segments across different levels, then measuring how detection amplitude and impulse response change as coherence drops.

The key test is whether the amplitude shows a sharp knee below some critical SNR threshold—indicating lock loss—or scales proportionally with coherence strength. I need to compare these two models statistically using segmented regression versus linear proportionality, with AIC/BIC or bootstrap methods to determine which fits better. The curvature ratio A(0.5)/A(1.0) becomes the discriminator: proportional scaling predicts a ratio near 0.5, while a threshold model predicts something lower if the critical point sits above 0.5.

A cleaner approach is to fit a power-law model A(s) = a·s^γ and use the exponent γ as the single pinned parameter. If γ > 1, the behavior is superlinear and threshold-like; γ ≈ 1 indicates proportional scaling; γ < 1 suggests saturation. I'll bootstrap the confidence interval on γ to test significance. I should also extract the lock time constant τ from the PRBS impulse response across different SNR levels, since frame-based locking predicts τ inversely proportional to SNR.

The core claim is that the register dynamically tracks evidence according to the schedule, with AC detection tied to membership-specific cues. The capture threshold becomes the key falsifiable prediction. I'm planning a multi-arm experiment: a main condition with PRBS schedule and membership evidence against neutral filler, a control with class-word non-membership, and an SNR sweep across the main condition.

The statistics I'm tracking are the PRBS cross-correlation peak energy, the membership-specificity contrast between main and control, the transfer function lock-time behavior, and the SNR curve exponent as a gating mechanism for the novel prediction. If the cross-correlation and membership contrast both hold, the carrier-tracked verdict stands and the capture threshold qualifies the result.

Now I'm mapping out the other verdict branches: if the AC response is surface-borne without the membership contrast, that's the lexical-only trap; if there's no cross-correlation peak at all, the register becomes a presence detector and the modulation leg fails; and void states when the sanity check breaks. My prior expectation leans toward frames 0-2 with the presence detector active and the lexical trap as competing hypotheses.

I'm considering how to weight these branches—maybe NO-TRACK gets the highest prior mass, followed by LEXICAL-ONLY, then the carrier-tracked variants. But that assumes the SNR gate is reliable. A cleaner approach might be to build the primary decision tree on just the membership gates, then treat SNR and the knee as a secondary read that only applies if we're already in the carrier-tracked regime. I'll use a separate gate for that sub-split.

For the model setup, I'm working with qwen3-4b and need to ensure the register is present and centroids are initialized. The band layers span all 36 layers, with semantic centroids concentrated in the deeper layers. I'm thinking about whether to read per-layer and gate on the mean across the deep half, or just focus on layers where the type-gram family is clean. The runtime will force the specifics, so I'll freeze the intent now and let the amendment discipline handle surprises as they come.

I can probably reuse the capture machinery from the existing type_icl_tag module and its idempotency statement populations. Now I'm looking at how to define the coherent segments using the member statements.

For the CTRL arm, I need new templates that mention the class word without asserting membership of w—something like "The animal is far away" or "An animal was mentioned; the {w} is here." I'll design this at build time with lexical checks similar to G5, keeping the freeze-level spec where CTRL segments contain the class word exactly once but no membership predication, with token budgets matched.

The probe frame will be a fixed neutral sentence mentioning w, reading the register at the final w token or averaged across probe tokens. For the PRBS sequence, I'm looking at PRBS-7 with length 127.

Now I'm calculating the token budget: roughly 20 segments with evidence around 10 tokens and probe around 6 tokens gives about 2000 tokens total, which fits comfortably in qwen3-4b. I could use PRBS-6 (63) with more nonces to be safer. For power, I'm keeping 20 nonces with classes assigned alternately, using different cyclic shifts of the PRBS per nonce to preserve spectrum and decorrelate across nonces.

Runtime-wise, 20 nonces across MAIN, CTRL, and 2 extra SNR levels means 80 sequences at roughly 2k tokens each, with activation capture at probe positions across band layers.

The cost looks reasonable. Before locking this in, I need to verify some instrument details: what the centroids.npz structure actually represents (30 nodes—are these animal/vehicle centroids or kind axes?), how idempotency reads the register (it was using LICENSING via logits and surprisal differences, not activations directly), and which axis in gate space maps to animal-vs-vehicle classification…

```
def band_layers(n_layers: int) -> list[int]:
    return list(range(round(BAND_DEPTH[0] * n_layers),
                      round(BAND_DEPTH[1] * n_layers) + 1))


def signed_T(h: np.ndarray, axes: np.ndarray, labels: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Band-mean signed class-tag projection per nonce.

    h: (n, L, d) residuals at the licensing position, band layers only.
    axes: (L, d) unit class axes (animal - vehicle direction).
    labels: (n,) 0=animal 1=vehicle. Sign fixed by true class: own-class
    direction present ⟺ T>0."""
    proj = np.einsum("nld,ld->nl", h, axes)           # (n, L)
    sign = np.where(np.asarray(labels, int) == 0, 1.0, -1.0)
    return proj.mean(axis=1) * sign


def class_axes(h_members: np.ndarray, member_labels: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """(m, L, d) member residuals → (L, d) unit axes mean(A) - mean(V)."""
    lab = np.asarray(member_labels, int)
    ax = h_members[lab == 0].mean(axis=0) - h_members[lab == 1].mean(axis=0)
    norm = np.linalg.norm(ax, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
    return ax / np.clip(norm, 1e-12, None)


def subtag(t_a0: float, t_a1: float, t_a5: float, ti4_pass: bool) -> str:
    """Wire-contrast subtag (declared thresholds; AMBIGUOUS unless TI4)."""
    if not ti4_pass or not np.isfinite(t_a5):
        return "AMBIGUOUS"
    denom = t_a1 - t_a0
    if abs(denom) < 1e-12:
        return "AMBIGUOUS"
    r = (t_a5 - t_a0) / denom
    if r <= SUBTAG_LO:
        return "DELIVERY-FAILURE"
    if r >= SUBTAG_HI:
        return "TAG-INSUFFICIENT"
    return "AMBIGUOUS"


def compute_gates_icl(b: dict, rng: np.random.Generator, alpha: float = 0.05,
```

### Thinking

The type-gram centroids use combinator basis functions rather than animal or vehicle categories, so I need to rely on the T register from section 11 for the actual membership judgment—that's where the residual projection onto the class axes lives, built from real member captures with the appropriate band depth range. The full design is ready to lock in and send to Michael.

Now I'm setting up the core claim: the type judgment operates as a carrier-borne demodulation event that dynamically tracks evidence on the tape, not a static stored geometry. If modulating coherent membership evidence with a known schedule produces register response at that schedule, I should be able to detect it with AC detection at constant-surface probe frames. I'm using qwen3-4b as the substrate—it's register-present and read-only, no training needed. The construction cycles through evidence segments paired with fixed probe frames, using a PRBS-6 schedule to modulate between coherent member statements and incoherent filler statements, with cyclic shifts per nonce to preserve the spectrum while decorrelating.

For the control arm, I'm using class-word-present non-membership segments instead of coherent slots—the class word appears once but the target word isn't predicated, keeping it token-matched and membership-free. I'm also varying the SNR by introducing probabilistic filler within coherent slots at different strengths. The readout extracts a signed band-mean projection of the residual at the probe-frame token onto the real-member class axis, capturing the trace across all blocks and per-layer depth texture. Then I'm computing the impulse response by correlating the output signal with the modulation schedule across a window of delays.

For the detection statistic, I'm summing the early-lag impulse response energy (lags 0–3) using a signed sum rather than unsigned energy—this preserves directional information so coherent evidence pushes the own-class score higher. The null distribution comes from random cyclic shifts of the modulation schedule, keeping them far enough from the true lag window to avoid contamination while preserving the PRBS autocorrelation structure.

I'm then aggregating across nonces by computing the mean detection statistic under the same shift applied consistently, with shifts drawn independently per nonce and averaged across all draws. The validation gates check that the real own-class response exceeds the anti-class response, that the signal is finite, and that the PRBS schedule has proper autocorrelation. The critical gate is whether the mean detection statistic exceeds zero with p < 0.05 against the 10k shift-null distribution. The amplitude scaling exponent γ determines whether the response is threshold-flavored (γ > 1), proportional (γ ≈ 1), or compressive (γ < 1), though with only three data points this is more of a screening test than a definitive finding—if it flags something, I'd want a finer sweep to confirm. The null hypothesis treats γ = 1 as the baseline, tested via bootstrap. For the verdicts, if LK1 doesn't trigger, the register shows no response at the scheduled timing, just a DC presence detector without modulation. The evidence from prior experiments suggests dynamic tracking is plausible — idempotency showed licensing accumulates with coherent exposures, and the DC/AC distinction revealed step/ramp responses to evidence, which points toward LK1 having real odds. But I'm uncertain whether the T register decays when incoherent filler is present at probe frames.

If licensing persists without decay, then the response becomes a pure integrator — the system accumulates evidence monotonically with slow decay characteristics. This means even without explicit decay, the correlation structure spreads across lags in a way that detection D at early lags remains positive. A mean-removed running sum of PRBS correlates with the input signal across positive lags with slow decay, so summing the impulse response coefficients still yields a positive result. This gives LK1 a pass with a characteristic low-pass transfer function behavior. Now I'm reconsidering LK3.

The PRBS test is elegant precisely because it distinguishes between a tracker with a relatively flat frequency response and fast locking versus a pure integrator with 1/f behavior and no decay. I could gate on this distinction, but LK3 is really just advisory — the verdict about whether we're seeing an integrator or a tracker is more about texture than a hard pass/fail. I'll name it clearly in the read discipline: LK1 passes with "evidence-schedule-correlated register response," and LK3 reads the transfer function shape to identify what kind of behavior we're dealing with.

The honest risk here is lexical — class word attention could bleed into the probe frames, shifting residuals. But the probe frames are constant-surface and separated, so that's the exact mechanism CTRL is designed to measure. I'll keep the mass parameters as they are.

Now I'm sketching the runtime: the NO-TRACK condition acknowledges that frames 0-2 plus the T-at-probe signal might be too weak or noisy, especially since the T effect in the earlier experiment was right at the licensing position after evidence, whereas here the probes are downstream. For the actual computation, I'm looking at 4 sequence types (MAIN, CTRL, and 2 SNR variants) across 63 blocks each, with each block containing evidence (~12 tokens) and probe (~7 tokens) for roughly 1200 tokens per sequence. That's 80 forward passes total across 20 nonces, capturing residuals at the probe positions across band layers. On Qwen 3.4B with MPS acceleration, I'm estimating 1–3 seconds per sequence, so the whole run should take 3–5 minutes, plus time for the real-member axis capture across 8 members.

I also need to validate the planted worlds—synthetic data generators for different scenarios like TRACKER (filtered signal plus noise), THRESHOLD (convex activation), LEXICAL (both main and control divergences positive), NOTRACK (pure noise), and VOID (degenerate axis)—and then test the gate logic and PRBS statistics machinery against those traces. For the model harness, I'm checking whether the type_icl_tag configuration (signed_T, class_axes, band_layers) aligns with the band depth and band fraction parameters used in the actual run.

Now I'm pinning down that BAND_DEPTH should match what the instrument landed on (0.50, 0.85), capturing residuals through the verbum.jlens capture function. I'm also moving the type-as-modulation-scheme section to a new P-TYPE-LOCKIN section at the end of the document and queuing up the restack. The fold-in items are checking out—idempotency populations with coherent and incoherent statements, the PRBS upgrade, and the SNR knee all look solid. One remaining honesty check: the probe frame's tokenization of w from NONCE_CANDS needs to ensure stable token generation.…

## Assistant

All instruments verified — full design assembled. The type-gram centroids are combinator-basis (wrong register for this), but §11's **T register** (tag-transit, the instrument that *landed* s315: signed band-residual projection onto real-member class axes, L18–L30) is exactly the per-frame readout LOCKIN needs. Here's the freeze draft.

---

## 🎯 FREEZE PROPOSAL — §P-TYPE-LOCKIN+PRBS (the original frame's must-win)

**The claim (§1 core).** If the type judgment is a **demodulation event** (carrier lock), the register must track evidence *dynamically*: modulate coherent membership evidence on the tape with a known schedule → the register responds AT the schedule (AC detection). The presence-detector results (NF-GAUGE et al.) were the DC reading; this is the AC reading. Frame is 0–2; this leg is its remaining life.

**Substrate.** qwen3-4b, read-only (no wire, no training), MPS, est. ~5–10 min. Reuses (λ one_way, no fork): `type_icl_tag` (signed_T / class_axes / BAND_DEPTH 0.50–0.85 — the landed §11 instrument) · `idempotency` populations (coherent `_member_stmts` + incoherent matched fillers) · `type_write` constants · `verbum.jlens` capture · `dsp.nulls`.

**Construction (per nonce w, class c; 20 nonces from NONCE_CANDS).** Sequence of 63 blocks; each block = *evidence segment* + *fixed probe frame* (constant surface, e.g., `" The {w}."` — read residual at last w token, §11's licensing-feed position). Schedule m(t) ∈ {+1, −1}:
- **MAIN**: m = **PRBS-6** (length-63 LFSR, cyclic shift per nonce — spectrum-preserving decorrelation). +1 slots = coherent membership paraphrase (cycled); −1 slots = incoherent membership-free filler (token-budget-matched).
- **CTRL (lexical control — the s321/s322 lesson AT the gate)**: +1 slots = class-word-present NON-membership segments (class word once, w never predicated, token-matched). Detection here = surface/attention bleed, not judgment.
- **SNR arms**: MAIN at s ∈ {0.5, 0.25} — coherent slots carry a coherent segment with exact fraction s, else filler.

**Excitation ⊥ measurement:** evidence modulates; readout happens only at constant-surface probes — any f-content in y(t) must be carried by state, not probe surface.

**Demodulation (pinned).** y(t) = T at probe t, mean-removed (DC excluded by construction). Impulse response ĥ(τ) = (2/B)Σ y(t)·m(t−τ). Detection **D = Σ_{τ=0..3} ĥ(τ)** (signed — coherent evidence must *raise* own-class T). Null = **10k random non-trivial cyclic shifts of m** (preserves PRBS autocorrelation ≡ matched-range null), aggregated across nonces per draw.

**Gates.**
- **LK0 SANE (void)** — real-member axis forms (own > anti); PRBS autocorrelation verified; y finite.
- **LK1 AC-DETECTION (make-or-break)** — mean D > 0, p < 0.05 vs shift-null.
- **LK2 JUDGMENT-NOT-LEXICAL (make-or-break #2)** — D_MAIN > D_CTRL paired across nonces, p < 0.05.
- **LK3 TRANSFER-FUNCTION (advisory, never gates)** — ĥ(τ) shape, |H(f)|, lock-time (63% step response), per-layer depth profile. This is where PRBS pays: **tracker (flat-ish H, fast lock) vs pure integrator (1/f, no decay — what bare non-idempotent accumulation would produce)** — reported as texture, not gated.
- **LK4 CAPTURE-THRESHOLD (secondary, read only if LK1∧LK2)** — A(s) at s ∈ {0.25, 0.5, 1.0}; fit A ∝ s^γ, bootstrap CI. γ > 1 (CI excludes 1) = THRESHOLD-FLAVORED (the novel prediction); γ ≈ 1 = PROPORTIONAL; γ < 1 = COMPRESSIVE. Three points = honest **knee-screen**: licenses "threshold-flavored convexity," NOT "capture threshold proven" — a finer sweep is owed if it fires.

**Verdicts + a-priori (NOT tuned; mass on mundane — the frame has earned skepticism).**

| Verdict | Condition | Mass |
|---|---|---|
| NO-TRACK | ¬LK1 | 30 |
| LEXICAL-TRACK | LK1 ∧ ¬LK2 | 25 |
| CARRIER-TRACKED-PROPORTIONAL | LK1 ∧ LK2 ∧ γ≈1 (or LK4 unread-able) | 20 |
| CARRIER-TRACKED-THRESHOLD | LK1 ∧ LK2 ∧ γ>1 | 15 |
| VOID | LK0 fail | 10 |

**Read discipline (banked now).**
- **NO-TRACK** → the §1 modulation leg fails; frame goes 0–3 with its must-win spent — the frame is effectively DEAD absent a Michael-level revision. §Synthesis (sign/magnitude) is independent and unaffected.
- **LEXICAL-TRACK** → third instance of the surface-carrier pattern (s321, s322) — a finding about the register, not a frame win.
- **CARRIER-TRACKED-\*** → licenses *evidence-schedule-correlated, membership-specific register response at constant probes* — NOT "types are CDMA"; LK3 texture says tracker-vs-integrator. THRESHOLD additionally gives the frame its first distinctive novel-prediction win (knee-screen grade).
- Single model, one class pair (animal/vehicle), T-register grain — bounds named.

Then: build with `--validate` planted worlds (TRACKER / THRESHOLD / LEXICAL / NOTRACK / VOID) + smoke, run, close.
